					<description>The third post in our semantic-layer series builds a customer 360 across Amazon Redshift and Amazon Aurora with Strategy Mosaic, exposes it to a Strands agent on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore through the Mosaic MCP server, and enforces per-user row and column security end-to-end.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;This post is co-written with Zhili Cheng, Principal Product Manager, George Earle, Director, AI Engineering, and Kyler K, Sales Engineer of Strategy.&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/build-a-semantic-layer-for-agentic-ai-on-aws-with-stardog-and-amazon-bedrock-agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;first post in this series&lt;/a&gt;, we built a semantic layer on AWS using Stardog’s knowledge graph over &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/rds/aurora/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Aurora&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/redshift/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Redshift&lt;/a&gt; and ran a Strands agent on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt; against that layer. The &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/build-a-semantic-layer-for-agentic-ai-on-aws-with-denodo-and-amazon-bedrock-agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;second post&lt;/a&gt; kept the same customer 360 (C360) pattern and swapped in Denodo’s data virtualization platform, extending the source mix to include an on-premises PostgreSQL cluster. This post uses the same C360 pattern again, with Strategy Mosaic as the semantic layer.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Stardog’s semantic layer is ontology-based: concepts and relationships modeled as RDF, with IRIs as the unit of federation and SPARQL as the query language. Denodo’s is data virtualization: base and derived views composed in VQL. Mosaic’s is dimensional: attributes, hierarchies, facts, and metrics composed through Strategy’s metric expression syntax, with linked models as the unit of federation. That model serves four native protocols from one definition: Model Context Protocol (MCP) for AI agents, JDBC/ODBC over Trino for SQL clients, XMLA for Power BI and Excel, and REST for custom applications. Behind those protocols is a &lt;a href="https://software.strategy.com/support/drivers-and-connectors" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;long list of certified data sources&lt;/a&gt;. Supported systems include cloud warehouses such as Snowflake and Databricks. Operational databases include &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/rds/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Relational Database Service (Amazon RDS)&lt;/a&gt;, Oracle, and SQL Server. Analytical engines include &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/athena/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Athena&lt;/a&gt;, Trino, and SAP HANA, and software as a service (SaaS) sources include Salesforce. The linked-model pattern extends beyond the Amazon Redshift and Amazon Aurora pair this walkthrough uses. In this walkthrough, a Strands agent hosted on AgentCore runtime, a capability of &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt; AgentCore, reaches Mosaic through the MCP endpoint. The joins, metric definitions, and per-caller row and column filters live inside Mosaic, not in the agent prompt.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We assume you’re familiar with the semantic-layer architecture from the first post in the series. If any of it is new, the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/build-a-semantic-layer-for-agentic-ai-on-aws-with-stardog-and-amazon-bedrock-agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Stardog post&lt;/a&gt; covers the ground.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;By the end of this post, you will know:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;How to model a C360 domain in Strategy Mosaic across Amazon Redshift and Amazon Aurora using two linked models, with no ETL.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;How Mosaic’s metric syntax (base, advanced with named filter objects, level qualifiers, and compound) captures the business definitions the agent must reason over.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;How to expose the model to an AgentCore-hosted Strands agent through the Mosaic MCP server, and how user identity propagates so per-user Row-Level Security (RLS) and Object-Level Security (OLS) hold end-to-end.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;How to choose between live pushdown and in-memory execution for agent traffic.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;How to publish the same governed definitions to Power BI, Tableau, notebooks, and custom apps, and how to export them in Apache Ossie format for tools outside Mosaic.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The source of truth for the walkthrough is &lt;a href="https://www2.microstrategy.com/producthelp/Current/NextGenAI/en-us/Content/agent_MCPServerIntegration.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strategy One (March 2026)&lt;/a&gt;, the release when Mosaic access through the MCP server became available by default.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="strategy-mosaic-terms-used-in-this-post"&gt;Strategy Mosaic terms used in this post&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A short reference before we get to the model.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;a href="https://software.strategy.com/blog/strategy-mosaic-unlock-decades-of-business-context" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Mosaic Schema and Mosaic Models&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/strong&gt; Two modeling paths within one universal semantic layer. Mosaic Schema is the centrally governed enterprise model. Mosaic Models is the self-service, AI-assisted modeling path in Mosaic Studio. Models can map to Schema attributes and inherit dimensions, object ACLs, and RLS. This walkthrough uses Mosaic Models.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;a href="https://www2.microstrategy.com/producthelp/Current/Mosaic/en-us/Content/mosaic_logical_modeling.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Model&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/strong&gt; The top-level artifact within either modeling path. Publishes attributes, metrics, hierarchies, and security rules over physical tables. The walkthrough uses two Mosaic Models, one over Amazon Redshift and one over Aurora, linked together.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;a href="https://www2.microstrategy.com/producthelp/Current/Mosaic/en-us/Content/attributes_in_model.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Attribute&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/strong&gt; A business entity such as Customer. Carries multiple forms: an ID form for joining and a display form for reading. Mosaic uses forms to resolve names like “Carolyn Inworth” to the underlying &lt;code&gt;cid&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;a href="https://www2.microstrategy.com/producthelp/Current/Mosaic/en-us/Content/Hierarchy_Editor_in_model.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Hierarchy&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/strong&gt; A parent-child relationship between attributes. Category rolls up through parent to Department. Address rolls up to City to State.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;a href="https://www2.microstrategy.com/producthelp/Current/Mosaic/en-us/Content/advanced_metrics_in_model.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Fact and metric&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/strong&gt; A fact is a numeric column. A metric aggregates it. &lt;code&gt;Purchase Amount = price * quantity&lt;/code&gt; is a base metric. Metrics can also be advanced (an aggregation qualified by a named condition) or compound (a formula over other metrics).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;a href="https://www2.microstrategy.com/producthelp/Current/FunctionsRef/Content/FuncRef/Filter_expressions.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Filter object&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/strong&gt; A named condition referenced from metrics in angle brackets. &lt;code&gt;[Large Orders]&lt;/code&gt; is a filter whose qualification is &lt;code&gt;Purchase Amount &amp;gt; 500&lt;/code&gt;. A metric references &lt;code&gt;&amp;lt;[Large Orders]&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt; without inlining the predicate.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;a href="https://www2.strategy.com/producthelp/Current/AdvancedReportingGuide/WebHelp/Lang_1033/Content/Level_metrics__Modifying_the_context_of_data_calcu.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Level qualifier&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/strong&gt; Metric expression syntax that pins aggregation grain. &lt;code&gt;{~+, !Customer+}&lt;/code&gt; sets the grain to Customer regardless of how the query is sliced.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;a href="https://www2.microstrategy.com/producthelp/Current/Mosaic/en-us/Content/mosaic_time_analytics.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Model linking&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/strong&gt; Composition across models on shared attributes (Customer, Time). Mosaic pushes aggregates to each source and joins intermediate results on the linking attributes.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;a href="https://www2.microstrategy.com/producthelp/Current/NextGenAI/en-us/Content/agent_MCPServerIntegration.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Certified model&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/strong&gt; Only certified models are reachable through the MCP server.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;a href="https://www2.microstrategy.com/producthelp/current/workstation/en-us/Content/manage_intelligent_cubes.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Live or in-memory&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/strong&gt; Two per-model execution modes: live pushdown to the source, or answers from a Mosaic-resident cube. Details later in this post.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="the-example-customer-360-across-amazon-redshift-and-amazon-aurora"&gt;The example: Customer 360 across Amazon Redshift and Amazon Aurora&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The C360 pattern from the first two posts stays in place. The schema is adapted from &lt;a href="https://www.stardog.com/blog/stardog-knowledge-kits-your-path-to-mastery-in-knowledge-graphs/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Stardog’s C360 Knowledge Kit&lt;/a&gt;, so a reader following the series sees the same entities, keys, and anchor question answered by a different semantic layer. A retail organization has customer records, addresses, credit cards, and rewards enrollment sitting on the operational side, and orders, products, and product categories sitting in the warehouse. Business users want answers that span both without knowing which system holds which fact.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Where this post differs is how Mosaic reaches the two sources. Mosaic does not federate rows across engines at query time. It plans one query per source, pushes native SQL down, and joins the intermediate result sets in the Mosaic engine on the shared attributes declared through model linking. The C360 walkthrough models the analytical side as a Purchase model over Amazon Redshift and the operational side as a Customer model over Amazon Aurora PostgreSQL-compatible edition.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The schema across the two databases:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Amazon Aurora PostgreSQL-compatible (operational)&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Table&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Key columns&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;customer&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;cid&lt;/code&gt; (PK), &lt;code&gt;first_name&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;last_name&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;email&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;ssn&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;phone&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;location&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;address&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;id&lt;/code&gt; (PK), &lt;code&gt;city&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;state&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;zip&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;street_name&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;credit_card&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;id&lt;/code&gt; (PK), &lt;code&gt;cid&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;card_num&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;card_type&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;rewards_account&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;id&lt;/code&gt; (PK), &lt;code&gt;cid&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;account_id&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;create_date&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Amazon Redshift (analytical)&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Table&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Key columns&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;purchase&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;id&lt;/code&gt; (PK), &lt;code&gt;cid&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;pid&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;date&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;quantity&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;price&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;card&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;product&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;id&lt;/code&gt; (PK), &lt;code&gt;name&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;brand&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;price&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;dept&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;category&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;id&lt;/code&gt; (PK), &lt;code&gt;dept_name&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;parent&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;vendor&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;id&lt;/code&gt; (PK), &lt;code&gt;vendor_name&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;industry&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The shared ID is &lt;code&gt;cid&lt;/code&gt;, an integer that appears as the primary key in &lt;code&gt;customer&lt;/code&gt; on Aurora and as a plain column in &lt;code&gt;purchase&lt;/code&gt; on Amazon Redshift. There is no SQL constraint linking them, and there can’t be, because they live in different engines. Every question that joins a customer to their orders must reconcile that integer somewhere. Mosaic absorbs it through model linking rather than a materialized cross-engine join.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A representative question that exercises the whole model: &lt;em&gt;Which of our top 20 customers by lifetime spend haven’t enrolled in the rewards program yet?&lt;/em&gt; This is the auto-enrollment question from the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/build-semantic-layer-denodo-amazon-bedrock-agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Denodo post&lt;/a&gt;. It needs customer profile from Aurora, aggregated spend from Amazon Redshift, and enrollment status from Aurora, ranked and filtered on definitions that live in the semantic layer.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="reference-architecture"&gt;Reference architecture&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Figure 1 shows the end-to-end runtime. The inbound authentication path runs from the client through AgentCore Gateway, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, and the runtime. The outbound query path runs from the Strands agent through the Mosaic MCP endpoint to Amazon Redshift and Amazon Aurora.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-21332-1-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-21332-1-1.png" alt="Reference architecture. Inbound authentication path and outbound query path between the client, Amazon Bedrock AgentCore components, the Strategy Mosaic MCP endpoint, the Mosaic engine, and Amazon Redshift plus Amazon Aurora." width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: Reference architecture. Inbound authentication path and outbound query path between the client, Amazon Bedrock AgentCore components, the Strategy Mosaic MCP endpoint, the Mosaic engine, and Amazon Redshift plus Amazon Aurora.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The runtime splits into an inbound path and an outbound path.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Inbound.&lt;/strong&gt; A client sends the user’s question through Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Gateway. Gateway validates the JSON Web Token (JWT) that identifies the human user against the enterprise identity provider (IdP). It then routes the request to Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runtime, which hosts the Strands agent. The agent invokes Anthropic Claude Sonnet 4.6 on Amazon Bedrock to interpret the prompt and plan the query.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Outbound.&lt;/strong&gt; When the agent decides to query the semantic model, its MCP tool connects directly to the Mosaic MCP endpoint at &lt;code&gt;https://&amp;lt;env-domain&amp;gt;/collaboration/mcp/mosaic&lt;/code&gt; over HTTPS. The agent presents an OAuth 2.0 access token in the &lt;code&gt;Authorization: Bearer&lt;/code&gt; header. Mosaic validates the token against its bundled OAuth authorization server and resolves the caller to a Strategy user. It then applies the &lt;code&gt;Use MCP Server&lt;/code&gt; privilege and certified-model gate, evaluates row-level and column-level security for that user, and plans the query across the linked Purchase and Customer models. Amazon Redshift and Amazon Aurora respond to source-native SQL that the Mosaic engine emits per source.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Two properties matter for the rest of the walkthrough. Cross-source joins happen because a modeler declared a link, not because two sources happen to be reachable. The agent can only traverse links that exist in the model. The token that reaches Mosaic represents the human user, not a service account, so RLS and OLS evaluate against the caller’s identity end-to-end. &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/secrets-manager/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Secrets Manager&lt;/a&gt; holds the caller’s refresh token between calls, and the Governed access section walks the full identity chain.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="build-the-semantic-layer"&gt;Build the semantic layer&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The walkthrough uses two Mosaic Models, the self-service AI-assisted modeling path in Mosaic Studio, linked on shared attributes. Mosaic Studio is the authoring suite, and the screenshots in this post come from its Design Center modeling workspace. Three passes: datasets and joins on each side, attributes and hierarchies, then metrics that carry the business logic. A team with an existing centrally governed &lt;a href="https://software.strategy.com/blog/strategy-mosaic-unlock-decades-of-business-context" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Mosaic Schema&lt;/a&gt; can map the model’s attributes to it and inherit dimensions, object ACLs, and RLS from the enterprise schema without a parallel security review. Do this early if your data architects already maintain a governed schema.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="two-linked-models"&gt;Two linked models&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Each Mosaic model connects to one live source. Model linking composes them.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Purchase&lt;/strong&gt;: Live against Amazon Redshift. Facts on the &lt;code&gt;purchase&lt;/code&gt; table (&lt;code&gt;price * quantity&lt;/code&gt;), dimensions on &lt;code&gt;product&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;category&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;vendor&lt;/code&gt;, and the purchase date.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Customer&lt;/strong&gt;: Live against Amazon Aurora PostgreSQL-compatible. Dimensions on &lt;code&gt;customer&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;address&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;credit_card&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;rewards_account&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Inside each model, the physical joins are declared once against the source schema. The Purchase model resolves &lt;code&gt;purchase.pid = product.id&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;product.dept = category.id&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;product.brand = vendor.id&lt;/code&gt;, plus a self-reference on &lt;code&gt;category.parent = category.id&lt;/code&gt; for the department rollup. The Customer model resolves &lt;code&gt;customer.location = address.id&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;customer.cid = credit_card.cid&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;customer.cid = rewards_account.cid&lt;/code&gt;. These are the relationships Mosaic uses to plan the source-native SQL it pushes down. Nothing physical crosses between Purchase and Customer.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The two models link on Customer and Time. When a question needs both (for example &lt;em&gt;top spenders in Wisconsin&lt;/em&gt; pulls state from &lt;code&gt;address&lt;/code&gt; and spend from &lt;code&gt;purchase&lt;/code&gt;), Mosaic pushes the appropriate aggregation to each source and combines the results on the linking attributes. No ETL, no third copy.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Domain ownership follows the model boundary. The CRM team owns Customer, and the data system team owns Purchase. Neither hands over its source. They agree on the linking attributes, and security propagates from the underlying models automatically. There’s no materialized cross-engine join and no shared physical schema between Amazon Redshift and Amazon Aurora. The join is the linking-attribute agreement.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="attributes-and-hierarchies"&gt;Attributes and hierarchies&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Attributes carry multiple forms. Customer uses &lt;code&gt;cid&lt;/code&gt; as the ID form and &lt;code&gt;first_name + last_name&lt;/code&gt; as the display form, so an agent can ask about “Carolyn Inworth” and Mosaic resolves it to the right key on both sides of the link. Figure 2 shows the Customer attribute authored in Mosaic Studio, with cid as the ID form and Full Name, First Name, and Last Name as display forms.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-21332-2-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-21332-2-1.png" alt="Customer attribute definition in Design Center. cid is the ID form used to join on the underlying key. Full Name, First Name, and Last Name are display forms." width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 2: Customer attribute definition in Design Center. cid is the ID form used to join on the underlying key. Full Name, First Name, and Last Name are display forms.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Two hierarchies matter for C360:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;The Category hierarchy rolls up through parent to Department.&lt;/strong&gt; The &lt;code&gt;category.parent&lt;/code&gt; self-reference means a department-level question aggregates consistently.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Address rolls up through City to State in the same pattern.&lt;/strong&gt; That makes &lt;code&gt;State = 'WI'&lt;/code&gt; a single attribute qualification rather than a join the agent must construct.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Figure 3 shows the geography hierarchy authored in Design Center, chaining State through City and ZIP Code down to Customer.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-21332-3-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-21332-3-1.png" alt="Geography hierarchy in Design Center. Attributes chain from State through City, ZIP Code, and Location to Customer, with Rewards and phone attributes hanging off the customer node." width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 3: Geography hierarchy in Design Center for the Customer model. State rolls up through City and ZIP Code to Location, and Location links to Customer.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The agent asks for &lt;code&gt;State = 'WI'&lt;/code&gt;, not for &lt;code&gt;address.state&lt;/code&gt;. Physical column names don’t leave the model. Every consumer of the semantic layer sees the attribute form and its display forms, and the hierarchy carries the rollup automatically.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="metrics-base-advanced-compound-time-intelligence"&gt;Metrics: Base, advanced, compound, time-intelligence&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A base metric applies a group function to a fact. &lt;code&gt;Purchase Amount&lt;/code&gt; is &lt;code&gt;Sum(price * quantity)&lt;/code&gt; on the &lt;code&gt;purchase&lt;/code&gt; table.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Figure 4 shows the Purchase Amount base metric authored in Mosaic Studio: the formula Price * Quantity, with a description field that tells the agent what the metric means in business terms.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-21332-4-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-21332-4-1.png" alt="Purchase Amount metric authored in Design Center. The formula is Price * Quantity. The description tells the agent what the metric means in business terms." width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 4: Purchase Amount metric authored in Design Center. The formula multiplies price by quantity, and the description tells the agent what the metric means in business terms.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The interesting C360 concepts are advanced metrics: an aggregation qualified by a named condition. In Strategy, conditions are references to filter objects in angle brackets, not inline predicates. Filter objects are model objects with their own name, description, and permissions, so they are reusable and auditable across metrics that share a definition.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Define the filters once:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Filter object&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Qualification&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;[Large Orders]&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;Purchase Amount &amp;gt; 500&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;[Sports Category]&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;Category = "Sports"&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;[Rewards Members]&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;Rewards Account is not null&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;[Card Present]&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;Purchase Card is not null&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;[Big Spender]&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;Customer Lifetime Spend &amp;gt; 10000&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Each filter object carries a name, description, qualification, and access permissions. The shape a modeler authors or exports for &lt;code&gt;[Large Orders]&lt;/code&gt; looks like this:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-plaintext"&gt;name:          [Large Orders]
description:   Purchases whose net line amount exceeds $500.
               Referenced by Large Order Spend and any metric that
               scopes to high-value transactions.
qualification: Purchase Amount &amp;gt; 500
permissions:   read:   Everyone
               modify: Data Modelers&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Then reference the filters in metric expressions. Each of these is an advanced metric: a base aggregation qualified by a named filter.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-plaintext"&gt;Large Order Spend    Sum([Purchase Amount]) {~+} &amp;lt;[Large Orders]&amp;gt;
Sports Spend         Sum([Purchase Amount]) {~+} &amp;lt;[Sports Category]&amp;gt;
Rewards Spend        Sum([Purchase Amount]) {~+} &amp;lt;[Rewards Members]&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Figure 5 shows the Design Center view of an advanced metric with a filter condition applied. It uses the same authoring pattern as the preceding &lt;code&gt;Large Order Spend&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-21332-5-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-21332-5-1.png" alt="Advanced-metric authoring in Design Center, with the base formula and filter condition shown on the Breakdown tab." width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 5: Advanced-metric authoring in Design Center, showing the base formula and the filter condition referenced from the Large Orders filter object.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;Customer Lifetime Spend&lt;/code&gt; also needs a level qualifier, because lifetime means the whole history regardless of how the current question is sliced:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-plaintext"&gt;Customer Lifetime Spend    Sum([Purchase Amount]) {~+, !Customer+}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;code&gt;!&lt;/code&gt; before &lt;code&gt;Customer&lt;/code&gt; sets grouping to none, so the metric returns each customer’s full total on every row rather than collapsing to one row per customer. See &lt;a href="https://www2.strategy.com/producthelp/Current/AdvancedReportingGuide/WebHelp/Lang_1033/Content/Level_metrics__Modifying_the_context_of_data_calcu.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strategy Metric Level Symbols&lt;/a&gt; for the full syntax.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Notice that &lt;code&gt;[Big Spender]&lt;/code&gt; in the filter table qualifies on a metric expression rather than an attribute predicate. Filter objects can gate on either. Share of wallet is a compound metric, built from other metrics:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-plaintext"&gt;Sports Share of Wallet    [Sports Spend] / [Customer Lifetime Spend]&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Levels and conditions carry through from the components unchanged. Share of wallet is a ratio, so it doesn’t add or average. The share across an AWS Region isn’t the average of each customer’s share, and the metric description tells the agent that. See &lt;a href="https://www2.strategy.com/producthelp/Current/AdvancedReportingGuide/WebHelp/Lang_1033/Content/Conditional_metrics.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strategy Conditional Metrics&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Beyond base, advanced, and compound, Mosaic ships governed time-intelligence metrics as first-class expressions. &lt;code&gt;Previous Year&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;Previous Quarter&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;Year-to-Date&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;Quarter-to-Date&lt;/code&gt; are declared once on the model and preserved through slice, so a question like &lt;em&gt;how does Sports spend this year compare with last year&lt;/em&gt; becomes an attribute pick and two metric references rather than a hand-written SQL date filter. Custom calendars attach at the model level, so the same year-over-year expression works for a fiscal year that ends in September and a calendar year that ends in December. See &lt;a href="https://software.strategy.com/blog/time-intelligence-metrics-in-mosaic-year-over-year-and-period-to-date-without-the-sql" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Governed Time-Intelligence Metrics in Mosaic&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Edit the filter object once and the Power BI dashboard, the SQL extract, and the agent all move together. That is what a named, governed model artifact gets you that an inline SQL predicate cannot.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="how-mosaic-plans-and-executes-queries"&gt;How Mosaic plans and executes queries&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The Mosaic engine parses each MCP &lt;code&gt;query&lt;/code&gt; call as Trino-dialect SQL, resolves the dimensional references to the underlying Purchase and Customer models, applies the RLS or OLS that the caller is subject to, and plans physical SQL per source. The dialect matches what Strategy publishes for the MCP &lt;a href="https://www2.microstrategy.com/producthelp/Current/NextGenAI/en-us/Content/agent_MCPServerIntegration.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;query tool&lt;/a&gt;, so the SQL the agent emits is portable across MCP clients connecting to the same certified model.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For the auto-enrollment question, planning unfolds like this:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Push down to Amazon Aurora: a join of &lt;code&gt;customer&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;rewards_account&lt;/code&gt; on &lt;code&gt;cid&lt;/code&gt;, projecting profile and &lt;code&gt;rewards_account.create_date&lt;/code&gt;, filtered where enrollment is null. Returns one row per unenrolled customer.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Push down to Amazon Redshift: &lt;code&gt;SUM(price * quantity)&lt;/code&gt; grouped by &lt;code&gt;cid&lt;/code&gt;. Returns one row per customer with total spend.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Combine inside Mosaic: join the two intermediate result sets on &lt;code&gt;cid&lt;/code&gt; (the linking attribute), rank by lifetime spend, and return the top 20.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Aurora runs a transactional profile join. Amazon Redshift runs the fact aggregation. Mosaic handles the cross-engine join on a small intermediate set (one row per customer, not one row per order). A hand-written pipeline approximates the same shape and then must be kept in sync forever.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;One SQL detail matters. Metrics are governed values, but Trino treats them as selected columns, so when a query uses &lt;code&gt;GROUP BY&lt;/code&gt;, metrics need an aggregation wrapper such as &lt;code&gt;SUM(metric_name)&lt;/code&gt;. The metric’s definition, conditions, and join paths are still resolved by Mosaic. The outer aggregation is the caller’s. A non-additive ratio like share of wallet therefore needs to say so in its description.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="live-pushdown-or-in-memory"&gt;Live pushdown or in-memory&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Every Mosaic model runs in one of two modes.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Mode&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Behavior&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Best fit&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Live&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Pushdown to Redshift or Aurora at query time&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Consistently-current data. Source security applies directly&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;In-memory&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Answered from a Mosaic-resident cube&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Bounded recent windows. Predictable latency. No per-query warehouse compute&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Both modes enforce the same security rules. RLS and OLS are set once on the model and evaluated regardless of where the answer comes from. The choice is a performance and cost question.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Agent traffic is repetitive and bursty. An agent decomposing &lt;em&gt;top spenders in Wisconsin&lt;/em&gt; into several exploratory follow-ups fires many small queries over the same recent slice. Serving that from memory turns variable per-query warehouse spend into fixed Mosaic capacity, and it’s usually faster. For interactive agent workloads over a bounded window, in-memory is often both quicker and cheaper. Configure Mosaic-side entitlements before switching a model to in-memory, and validate them in the same test pass you use to validate the numbers.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="one-definition-four-interfaces"&gt;One definition, four interfaces&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The metrics, attributes, hierarchies, and security rules built in the previous section reach every consumer through native protocols. No connector translation, no metric re-implementation, no drift.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Protocol&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Typical consumer&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What it uses Mosaic for&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;MCP&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;AI agents (Strands, Claude, Copilot, ChatGPT, Gemini)&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Semantic discovery + governed query&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;SQL / JDBC / ODBC (Trino)&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Tableau, SQL clients, Python notebooks&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Flexibility and ad-hoc analysis&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;DAX / XMLA&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Power BI, Excel PivotTables&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Semantic fidelity for BI&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;REST&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Custom applications, data science&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Programmatic access&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The same certified Mosaic model that the Strands agent in this walkthrough consumes also serves a business analyst working in &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/quick/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Quick&lt;/a&gt;, which registers Mosaic MCP as an OAuth-authenticated connector. No second integration is required.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="governed-access-identity-propagation-and-runtime-enforcement"&gt;Governed access: Identity propagation and runtime enforcement&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Mosaic enforces access in the semantic layer, before a query reaches Amazon Redshift or Aurora. HR and fraud roles see personally identifiable information (PII) fields like &lt;code&gt;ssn&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;card_num&lt;/code&gt;. Marketing and analytics roles do not. Enforcement is identical whether the caller is a Strands agent over MCP, an analyst in Excel over XMLA, or a notebook over JDBC. One set of RLS and OLS objects, one certified-model gate, one privilege check. Attributes and metrics marked as sensitive also feed &lt;a href="https://www2.microstrategy.com/producthelp/Current/Mosaic/en-us/Content/sentinel_risk_management.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Mosaic Sentinel Risk Management&lt;/a&gt;, which alerts on queries that touch flagged objects and detects behavioral anomalies as a defense-in-depth layer next to OLS.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Figure 6 shows the identity propagation chain across two lanes: the front-door OAuth handshake that runs once per user, and the per-request path that runs on every agent tool call. Each lane carries the user’s identity end-to-end so Mosaic can enforce RLS and OLS on the caller rather than a service account.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-21332-6.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-21332-6.png" alt="Identity propagation chain from the human user through the enterprise IdP, Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, AWS Secrets Manager, and the Mosaic MCP endpoint. The front-door OAuth handshake runs once per user (steps 1-4). The per-request path runs on every agent tool call (steps 5-10)." width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 6: Identity propagation chain. The front-door OAuth handshake runs once per user, or on refresh-token renewal (steps 1-4). The per-request path runs on every agent tool call (steps 5-10). Nothing in the chain re-scopes to a service account. Mosaic evaluates governance on the caller’s Strategy user identity.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The chain follows two lanes.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Front-door OAuth handshake (once per user).&lt;/strong&gt; The human user authenticates against the enterprise IdP. The application starts an OAuth 2.0 Authorization Code flow against Strategy Library at &lt;code&gt;/collaboration/authorize&lt;/code&gt; with scope &lt;code&gt;offline_access&lt;/code&gt;. Strategy Library, federated to the same IdP through SAML or OIDC, returns a refresh and access token pair at &lt;code&gt;/collaboration/token&lt;/code&gt;. The app persists the refresh token to AWS Secrets Manager, keyed by the user’s IdP sub claim.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Per-request path (every agent tool call).&lt;/strong&gt; AgentCore Gateway validates the IdP JWT and routes to AgentCore runtime, which hosts the Strands agent invoking Claude Sonnet 4.6 on Amazon Bedrock. The agent’s MCP tool retrieves the user’s refresh token from Secrets Manager, exchanges it for a fresh access token, and presents &lt;code&gt;Authorization: Bearer &amp;lt;access_token&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt; to &lt;code&gt;/collaboration/mcp/mosaic&lt;/code&gt;. Mosaic resolves the token to a Strategy user, enforces the &lt;code&gt;Use MCP Server&lt;/code&gt; privilege, certified-model gate, and RLS and OLS, then plans and executes against Purchase and Customer. A marketing analyst doesn’t see &lt;code&gt;ssn&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code&gt;card_num&lt;/code&gt;. A fraud analyst does.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Nothing in the chain re-scopes to a service account. Governance evaluates on the caller’s identity from IdP to Mosaic engine.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="expose-the-layer-through-the-mosaic-mcp-server"&gt;Expose the layer through the Mosaic MCP server&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Strategy Library exposes two MCP endpoints:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Endpoint&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Path&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What it does&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Agents MCP&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;/collaboration/mcp/agent&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Routes a question to a certified Strategy agent, which answers using its own data and instructions&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Mosaic MCP&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;/collaboration/mcp/mosaic&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Queries Mosaic models and AI-enabled cubes directly, no Strategy agent required&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Both work independently. This walkthrough uses &lt;a href="https://www2.microstrategy.com/producthelp/Current/NextGenAI/en-us/Content/agent_MCPServerIntegration.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Mosaic MCP&lt;/a&gt;. Putting the semantic layer directly in front of the Strands agent skips an inference hop.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="the-tool-chain"&gt;The tool chain&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Four tools, chained from project to model to semantics to query.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Tool&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What it does&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Parameters&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;get_projects&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Lists Strategy projects the caller can access&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;none&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;get_models&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Lists certified Mosaic models and datasets in a project&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;project&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;get_semantics&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Returns a model’s full definition: attributes and metrics with data types, descriptions, and metric formulas&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;model&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;query&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Runs a read-only Trino SQL query. Writes and DDL rejected with &lt;code&gt;MOSAIC_SQL_WRITE_REJECTED&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;sql&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The agent chains these calls automatically. &lt;code&gt;get_projects&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;get_models&lt;/code&gt; narrow to the right model by description before the agent reads a column name. &lt;code&gt;get_semantics&lt;/code&gt; returns the governed definitions the agent then uses to compose SQL against real object names: &lt;code&gt;Sports Share of Wallet&lt;/code&gt; as a compound metric, &lt;code&gt;Customer Lifetime Spend&lt;/code&gt; scoped to Customer, the Category hierarchy, and the formula for every metric.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Two Mosaic-specific SQL conventions matter. Column identifiers follow a &lt;code&gt;&amp;lt;form&amp;gt;__&amp;lt;name&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt; pattern and must be wrapped in double quotes (&lt;code&gt;"customer__customer name"&lt;/code&gt;). Time attributes are exposed both as base columns and as pre-bucketed interval columns (&lt;code&gt;(day interval)&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;(month interval)&lt;/code&gt;), and the docs explicitly say to use the interval columns for time-grouped aggregations rather than &lt;code&gt;date_trunc&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="metadata-is-what-makes-it-accurate"&gt;Metadata is what makes it accurate&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;get_semantics&lt;/code&gt; returns definitions, but the agent still must choose the right metric. Without descriptions, &lt;code&gt;Total Spend&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;Customer Lifetime Spend&lt;/code&gt; look interchangeable, and they aren’t. Four properties do most of the work: object descriptions (what the metric means and when not to use it, including aggregation behavior for non-additive metrics), semantic definitions (how compound metrics compose), relationships (which attributes are valid slices), and governance context (so the agent can say what it can’t see rather than presenting a filtered number as complete). The model description is the first thing &lt;code&gt;get_models&lt;/code&gt; returns and it’s how the agent picks the right model. Mosaic can import metadata from upstream catalogs, so column descriptions already maintained flow in rather than being retyped.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="deploy-the-agent"&gt;Deploy the agent&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Strands is a small framework for building agents. A short Python entry point wires Claude Sonnet 4.6 on Amazon Bedrock to the Mosaic MCP endpoint as a tool source. The AgentCore runtime hosts the process. AgentCore Gateway terminates inbound requests and forwards the human user’s JWT.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The system prompt matters. Give the agent explicit instructions to follow the three-step call pattern before writing SQL: &lt;code&gt;get_projects&lt;/code&gt;, then &lt;code&gt;get_models&lt;/code&gt;, then &lt;code&gt;get_semantics&lt;/code&gt; on the target model. Tell it to reference only attribute and metric names that &lt;code&gt;get_semantics&lt;/code&gt; returned. Include a short section on the business terminology in the target model (&lt;code&gt;Customer Lifetime Spend&lt;/code&gt; is the customer’s total across their history regardless of the current slice. &lt;code&gt;Sports Share of Wallet&lt;/code&gt; is a ratio. And so on). This is the prompt engineering that pays the most in accuracy.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Turn on &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/prompt-caching.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock prompt caching&lt;/a&gt; for the reused portion of the system prompt. The &lt;code&gt;get_semantics&lt;/code&gt; payload runs to a few thousand tokens and doesn’t change within a model version, so caching drops the input-token cost per call on repeated invocations. Agent traffic against a stable semantic layer is exactly the repetitive, bursty pattern that benefits most.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Two effects compound on the MCP path. First, &lt;code&gt;get_semantics&lt;/code&gt; returns compressed business context (metric definitions, hierarchies, attribute forms) instead of raw schema DDL, so the model reasons over a smaller, higher-signal payload. Strategy’s &lt;a href="https://software.strategy.com/blog/your-ai-doesnt-need-more-tokens-it-needs-context" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;published benchmark&lt;/a&gt; measures the input-token and per-query cost impact of serving semantic context versus raw schema. Second, Bedrock prompt caching keeps that already-compressed payload warm across calls. For repetitive agent traffic, both effects apply per call: the model reasons over compressed context, and repeat calls hit the cache.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="verification"&gt;Verification&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To confirm the semantic layer, the agent, and the identity flow work as intended, we ran three query categories through the agent and then a governance rerun of one of them as a different role.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Query 1, Aurora-only.&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;em&gt;List our customers in Wisconsin, with their city.&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The agent generates a query against the Customer model that projects the display forms of Customer and City and filters state to WI:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-sql"&gt;SELECT "customer__customer name", "city__city name"
FROM   "Customer"
WHERE  "state__state name" = 'WI'
ORDER BY "customer__customer name"&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Mosaic pushes a single query to Aurora and returns the rows. Amazon Redshift is not touched.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Query 2, Amazon Redshift only.&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;em&gt;What are our top 10 products by units sold?&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-sql"&gt;SELECT "product__product name", SUM("Units Sold") AS "total_units"
FROM   "Purchase"
GROUP BY "product__product name"
ORDER BY "total_units" DESC
LIMIT 10&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The entire query pushes to Amazon Redshift. Aurora is not touched.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Query 3, cross-model.&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;em&gt;Which of our top 20 customers by lifetime spend haven’t enrolled in the rewards program yet?&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-sql"&gt;SELECT "customer__customer name",
       "state__state name",
       SUM("Customer Lifetime Spend") AS "lifetime_spend"
FROM   "Customer"
WHERE  "rewards_account__account_id" IS NULL
GROUP BY "customer__customer name", "state__state name"
ORDER BY "lifetime_spend" DESC
LIMIT 20&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Mosaic plans across both models. Profile and enrollment status come from Aurora, spend aggregation from Amazon Redshift, joined on the Customer linking attribute inside the Mosaic engine, then filtered and top-N’d.&amp;nbsp;The agent did not need to define &lt;code&gt;Customer Lifetime Spend&lt;/code&gt;. It referenced the metric by name and Mosaic resolved the formula, level qualifier, and linking paths.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Governance rerun.&lt;/strong&gt; We ran Query 1 with &lt;code&gt;ssn&lt;/code&gt; added to the projection as two callers. As a fraud analyst, &lt;code&gt;get_semantics&lt;/code&gt; returned the SSN attribute and the query returned the values. As a marketing analyst, &lt;code&gt;get_semantics&lt;/code&gt; returned a Customer attribute without &lt;code&gt;ssn&lt;/code&gt; at all, so the agent had no attribute to project. Same request, same query text, two correct answers from one definition.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Cross-interface confirmation.&lt;/strong&gt; We opened the same certified Customer model in Excel through the Mosaic XMLA endpoint and dragged &lt;code&gt;State&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;Customer Lifetime Spend&lt;/code&gt; onto a PivotTable. The state-level totals matched the agent’s numbers exactly.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Early in the run, the agent averaged share of wallet across customers, the classic non-additive mistake. Adding one line to the metric description (&lt;em&gt;“ratio metric. Do not average across customers, recompute from components”&lt;/em&gt;) stopped it. That’s the case for writing metric descriptions in the first sprint rather than the last.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="extending-across-data-sources"&gt;Extending across data sources&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The same linking mechanism extends to a third source: clickstream events in Amazon Simple Storage Service (&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/s3/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon S3&lt;/a&gt;) queried through Amazon Athena, tables in Apache Iceberg on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/s3/features/tables/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon S3 Tables&lt;/a&gt;, a capability of Amazon S3, or a SaaS source such as a CRM. Model the source in Mosaic, link it on Customer and Time, and browse-to-buy questions compose into the same customer view without a third pipeline. Adding a source doesn’t change the metric definitions that already exist. The agent can only travel paths a modeler declared. &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/lake-formation/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Lake Formation&lt;/a&gt; governs access to the underlying lake tables. Mosaic’s RLS and OLS apply on top.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The same live-source pattern applies to every system in &lt;a href="https://software.strategy.com/support/drivers-and-connectors" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strategy’s supported data source catalog&lt;/a&gt;, covering on-premises databases such as PostgreSQL and MySQL alongside the warehouses and engines already named in this post.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="interoperability-with-apache-ossie"&gt;Interoperability with Apache Ossie&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Some tools will not take a dependency on a single semantic layer. &lt;a href="https://ossie.apache.org/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Apache Ossie (incubating)&lt;/a&gt; is the open specification for that gap, launched as the Open Semantic Interchange on September 23, 2025 and now in the Apache Incubator as Apache Ossie (incubating). It defines a vendor-neutral format for metrics, dimensions, relationships, and business concepts so definitions move between tools without being retyped. Strategy participates alongside Snowflake, Dremio, dbt Labs, Salesforce, and others.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Mosaic exports and imports Ossie artifacts. Metric and dimension definitions modeled over Amazon Redshift publish as Ossie YAML consumable by Ossie-aware tools without a Mosaic connector. Definitions authored elsewhere (a metric maintained in dbt, a dimension standardized in a catalog) flow into Mosaic instead of being re-implemented. The artifacts are plain text, so they live in Git and promote through environments like other code.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Apache Ossie is early. Use it where portability matters most and fidelity matters least, for example formulas, dimensions, descriptions, and ownership metadata, while keeping governance policy, execution strategy, and security configuration native to Mosaic.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="deployment-strategy-cloud"&gt;Deployment: Strategy Cloud&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Mosaic runs as a managed service on Strategy Cloud. Strategy operates the application. Connections to Amazon Redshift and Amazon Aurora go over the network from Strategy Cloud egress. You allow-list the Strategy Cloud CIDRs on the source security groups.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="observability"&gt;Observability&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Two observability planes cover the path from question to answer.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Sentinel and Platform Analytics&lt;/strong&gt; cover the query side. Sentinel captures near real-time query duration, slow queries, and usage by user, tool, and model.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AgentCore Observability&lt;/a&gt;, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore,&lt;/strong&gt; covers the agent side: planning steps, tool calls, latency per turn. Sentinel and Platform Analytics show what happened once the request reached Mosaic. AgentCore Observability shows what the agent was doing before it got there.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Reliable agentic analytics combines strong reasoning, trusted business context, and secure execution. Amazon Bedrock provides the model, Strategy Mosaic provides the governed semantic layer over live data, and Amazon Bedrock AgentCore hosts the agent and terminates authentication. The agent talks to the semantic layer, not to Amazon Redshift or Aurora directly. Schemas evolve, the semantic model absorbs the change, and the same definitions serve Power BI, Tableau, notebooks, the Strands agent, and Amazon Quick. Through Apache Ossie, they also reach tools that do not connect to Mosaic.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="where-to-start"&gt;Where to start&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Pick one metric currently argued about (NRR, contribution margin, active customer are common candidates). One metric with more than one definition in circulation.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Write three to five business questions about it. These become your acceptance tests.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Model your primary source in Mosaic and get the metric returning a defensible number.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Link additional sources incrementally, one at a time, on the attributes that already exist in the model.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Author descriptions on every metric and attribute the agent will see. This is where accuracy moves most.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Connect the Strands agent over Mosaic MCP and run your acceptance questions.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Observe real query patterns for a couple of weeks, then move what’s hot to in-memory.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Export stable definitions to Apache Ossie and put them under version control.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt;
